Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月 12 日

1/2 
10 月企業物価指数 
～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  阿原 健一郎 
（℡：050-5470-4868）

1. 国内企業物価指数は幾分加速 
 本日公表された 10 月の国内企業物価指数は前年比＋7.1％と、前月（同＋6.8％）から 
伸びが幾分加速した。前年比寄与度で内訳を見ると、玄米や豚肉などの「農林水産物」のマイナ
ス寄与が前月から拡大した一方、「電力・都市ガス・水道」や「石油・石炭製品」、「化学製品」の
プラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表１）。 
 背景には、原油やナフサの価格上昇が石油製品や化学製品へ波及していることに加え、電力に
おいても、過去の輸入燃料価格の上昇がタイムラグを伴って燃料費調整単価に反映され、全体を
押し上げていることがある。農林水産物の価格下落が一部で下押し要因となっているものの、エ
ネルギー関連を中心としたコスト上昇圧力は依然として根強い。

２. 輸入物価指数はわずかに加速 
 10 月の輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.8％と、前月（同＋23.8％）から伸びがわずかに加速した。
「石油・石炭・天然ガス」のプラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表
２）。 
 為替変動の影響を除いた契約通貨ベースでも同＋19.2％と、前月（同＋17.3％）から伸びが拡大
しており、海外の資源価格上昇が輸入物価を押し上げている。輸入コストの増加が時間差を伴っ
て国内の製品価格に波及することを踏まえると、先行きも価格転嫁を通じて消費者物価への上昇
圧力が残る可能性がある。

以 上

りません。

巧新(1563 TT) Trading Buy

2026、 2027 年營運動能向上，投資建議 Trading Buy。  
投資建議 Trading Buy ：巧新(1563 TT) 努力進行轉型，半導體
新事業於 2H26 逐漸取得進展，且再生鋁出貨占比也明顯拉升，
帶動毛利率明顯改善。2026 年獲利表現回升且 2027 年獲利能
力續揚。惟考量近期汽車零組件族群評價不易拉升，投資建議
Trading Buy，目標價 76 元 (以 2027 年 EPS 及 PE 15 倍評價)。  
3Q26 本業獲利小幅增長，因業外收入挹注，整體獲利顯著季增；
預估 4Q26 獲利相對下降：3Q26 營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.83%、
YoY+18.48%。受惠於再生鋁採用比重提升且匯率相對有利，毛
利率由 2Q26 的 24.32%提升至 25.90%。但因運輸成本上漲，營
業費用同步增長，營業利益 QoQ+6.72%、 YoY+211.71%。因業
外認列大筆匯兌利益，稅率表現穩定。稅後純益 2.47 億元，
QoQ+247.56%、 YoY+478.06%， EPS 1.46 元。4Q26 方面，預估
營收 19.21 億元，QoQ-4.70%、 YoY+16.97%。獲利能力方面，
受惠於再生鋁採用率高，預估毛利率提升至 27.54%。因運費相
對下降，預估營業利益 QoQ+15.87%、 YoY+342.49%。因 3Q26
已認列大筆匯兌收益，若以保守角度看待 4Q26 業外貢獻，預估
稅後純益 1.67 億元，QoQ-32.69%、YoY 轉虧為盈，EPS 0.98 元。 
預估 2026 年獲利表現回升，2027 年獲利能力續揚：2026 年方
面，輪圈出貨小幅提升，而半導體相關零組件自 2H26 開始貢獻
初步營收。預估 2026 年營收 77.89 億元，YoY+11.63%。隨著再
生鋁採用率提升且營收規模增長，毛利率有望逐季提升，預估
2026 年毛利率提升至 24.97%，營業利益 YoY+43.13%。若業外
較無匯兌損失影響，預估稅後純益 6.16 億元，YoY+93.56%，
EPS 3.64 元。2027 年方面，根據車廠預示量，鋁圈出貨量將由
2026 年的 60 萬顆提升至 70 萬顆，有助於帶動車用產品營收增
長。而半導體營收占比有機會提升至 5~10%，將提供額外營收
動能。因再生鋁採用比重提升，且半導體產品毛利率相對較高，
2027 年毛利率及獲利能力有望優於 2026 年。預估 2027 年營收
92.77 億元，YoY+19.10%，稅後純益 8.56 億元，YoY+38.99%，
EPS 5.06 元。

2 群益投顧 
  巧新(1563 TT) ESG：  
Environmental 環境保護：(1)鋁下腳料 100%回收，製成再生鋁棒，持續循環
再利用。(2)導入各式節能設備具體節能，已降低溫室氣體排放量。(3)屏東
廠自建太陽能光電。Social 社會責任：(1)獲得 ASI(鋁業管理倡議)績效標準
認證。(2)定期進行人權盡職調查與評估，流程和政策涵蓋重要的人權問題，
保障員工和活動參與者的安全。(3)響應地方政府公益活動。Governance 公
司治理：(1)風險管理依公司程序辦理，內控嚴謹，並設有專職稽核部門定期
查核。(2)將誠信經營納入新人教育訓練課程與人力資源政策中，設立明確
有效之獎懲及申訴機制。(3)每年定期發布 ESG 報告書。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

日本：7月以降の設備投資需要はAI関連産業を中心に旺盛
3
 7～9月期の機械受注は増勢回復へ。AI関連業種からの受注がけん引
― ①旺盛なAI需要のもと、関連業種では設備不足感が強まっていたこと、②輸出増が持続するもとで、好業績が期待され
ること、③中東情勢の不確実性が後退したことなどが背景
 先行き設備投資は緩やかな増勢を辿る見込み
― 半導体製造装置をはじめ、AI・データセンター関連品目を中心に受注残が増加しており、目先こうした傾向が続く見通し
― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

世界：AI関連投資の拡大が鉱物需要を押し上げる一方、鈍い資源開発投資の回復ペース
4
 AI関連需要の拡大が銅やレアアースの需給環境に影響
― データセンター建設や電力インフラ整備に伴い、銅やレアアースの需要が拡大。ガリウム等の戦略鉱物への需要も増加
 資源開発投資の回復が遅れるなか、中長期的な供給不足への懸念強まる
―IEAは、2035年に銅が供給量対比で約26％の供給不足に陥ると予測。レアアースは供給不足を回避する見込みながら、

採掘・精錬において中国が支配的地位を占めており、供給元の集中がリスク

世界：ヘッジファンドのレバレッジ拡大が市場ショックを増幅、銀行部門への波及にも警戒
 高まるヘッジファンドの存在感と市場への影響力
― ヘッジファンドの総資産は2013年の約4兆ドルから2026年初には約13兆ドルに拡大。平時には市場の流動性や効率性
を支える一方、ストレス時にはレバレッジ解消や取引集中が価格変動を増幅させる懸念
 ヘッジファンドの損失がプライムブローカーを通じて銀行部門に波及するリスク
―IMFはヘッジファンドの運用成績悪化がプライムブローカーの収益にも悪影響を及ぼすと分析。銀行自身の健全性に加
え、ノンバンクとの相互連関を通じた金融システム全体への波及にも警鐘

世界：2026年の世界経済は底堅く推移も、経済成長の恩恵やインフレの負担は一部に偏在
 IMFは2026年の世界成長率を小幅に上方修正（3.0％→3.1％）
―AIブームを追い風とした半導体輸出の増加等を背景に、アジアを中心に上方修正
― 備蓄放出・代替調達等による政府・企業の対応が原油供給ショックによる下押しを緩和したことも影響
 一方、AI需要の取り込みや供給ショックへの耐性には国家間で格差
―IMFによれば、世界の経済成長の95％を占める国の数は54（4月）から32（10月）に減少したほか、今次改定では47％
の国が成長率を下方修正。AIブームの恩恵や供給ショックによる負担は一部の国・地域に偏在

11

 ASEANの消費は中東情勢緊迫化後に減速も、概して底堅く推移

― 消費マインドは中東情勢緊迫化後の悪化に歯止め。もっとも、中東危機の長期化が重しに

（出所） 各国統計、CEICより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み
（注） コメは玄米及び精米。食品関連は、飲食料品及び農林水産物（除くコメ）

（出所） 日本銀行「企業物価指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
ASMedia Technology Inc
Inventory adjustment weighs on N-T outlook
Q326 earnings broadly in line; PC demand remains weak
ASMedia reported Q326 EPS of NT$26.39, broadly in line with UBSe but above Street,
helped by ~NT$1.5bn of non-op income mainly from WT Micro. Q326 sales of NT
$2.66bn declined 2% QoQ and 33% YoY, reflecting continued weakness in PC demand
amid elevated memory prices and the shift at its major customer from AM5 back to the

lower-cost AM4 platform. Q326 GM came in at 51.6% (+1.3ppt QoQ), helped by a
more favorable product mix and pricing adjustments.  Opex increased to NT$795mn on
higher R&D spending including 12nm chip tape-out and a 10-15% increase in
headcount expansion, resulting in  OpM declining 2.1ppt QoQ to 21.7%.  Q326 sales mix
was 74% for host controllers, ~21% automotive and ~5%  device.
Sell-through improving, but  inventory adjustment to weigh through Q127F

ASMedia is seeing improving PC motherboard demand, with Q326 motherboard
production up >20% QoQ and management viewing H126 as the near-term trough.
However, elevated memory prices have driven AMD's product mix back toward the
lower-ASP AM4 platform (AM4  >70% of Q326 chipset shipments up from >50% in
Q226), while AMD continues to digest inventory. It noted AMD's sales contribution has
already declined from >50% of sales in H126 to <50% in Q326, and management

expects the inventory correction to continue through Q127, delaying the benefit from
improving motherboard sell-through. We expect October to mark the trough (monthly
sales down 43% MoM) and will remain muted on AMD's inventory control, resulting  in
Q426 sales  declining~33% QoQ  while GM should decline by 1.6ppt QoQ to 50.0% as
slower pull in of AM5 could lead to inventory provision, despite a reversal in H127E.
2027E recovery more back-end-loaded

ASMedia remains confident in retaining its sole-supplier position for AMD’s next-
generation Olympic (AM5.5) chipset platform, with contribution expected from H227
and ASP ~50-60% higher than AM4. We expect USB4 demand to recover alongside the
Olympic ramp, with additional upside from a new US robotics customer from H227.
Separately, we expect AM5 shipments to recover  and potential  inventory writebacks to
provide upside to GM. We believe PCIe packet-switch contribution could be more

gradual given slower design-in progress. Overall, we estimate ASMedia’s 2027E sales to
grow ~5% YoY (vs. previous 17% YoY expectations), with growth skewed to H227.
Valuation: Retain Buy with PT of NT$1,500
We lower our 2026/27/28E EPS by 2%/1%/2% primarily on softer PC demand and
inventory adjustment, offset by higher better earnings contribution from WT Micro. We
cut our PT to NT$1,500 (from NT$1,700), based on our SOTP valuation  (18x for core

earnings and 11.5x for non-core), implying 13x 2027E PE.  Maintain Buy rating as stock is
trading near trough 14x NTM PE  vs an 11-48x historical range, while its diversification
into non-PC   should support its topline/OP to recover from 2027E.

Company Description
ASMedia is a Taiwan-based fabless IC design house founded by parent company Asustek
Computer Inc. in 2004. ASMedia is dedicated to high-speed input/output (I/O) controllers,
such as USB, PCIe and SATA, and its products are mainly used in desktop PCs, motherboards

and add-on cards. In 2024, ASMedia generated 84% of its revenue from host controllers and
16% from device controllers. The company collaborates with Advanced Micro Devices (AMD)
to design southbridge chipsets for Zen-based desktop central processing units (CPUs). AMD is
the company's largest client account, contributing 58% of revenue in 2024.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target on sum-of-the-parts methodology. We value ASMedia's core and

non-core businesses based on a target PE multiple.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainty, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights.
We believe risks for ASMedia include: 1) AMD's chipset insourcing or other design houses
entering AMD's chipset business; 2) faster penetration of USB4 native support in chipsets; 3)

more entrants in the USB device controller market; 4) continued suspension of China tender
projects; and 5) increased US export controls impacting China's computing capability.

Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.

getting better, N/A = no view)

2. Is the regulatory/government environment facing the firm likely to improve or
deteriorate over the next six months? Rate on a scale of 1-5 (1 = getting tougher

3 = no change, 5 = getting better, N/A = no view)

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

### Page 1

E Ink Holdings

Cuts 2026 guidance again; PTFE mass 
production in 2028; reiterate Neutral

Reiterate Rating: NEUTRAL | PO: 148.00 TWD | Price: 140.00 TWD

Cuts ’26 sales guidance to +9% YoY; IoT as key ’27 driver  
Mgmt. cut its 2026 guidance again and now expects overall sales to +9% YoY (prior: up 
10-15% YoY; Jan-Oct sales +4% YoY). It expects IoT sales up 35%+ YoY (BofAe: +39% 
YoY, with ESL sales +32% and signage sales more than doubling) and CE sales -15% YoY. 
Mgmt. cited weaker CE demand and still expects signage at single-digit% of 2026 sales 
(BofAe: 7%). The latest full-year guidance implies a mid- to high single-digit% MoM 
decline in Nov and Dec. Mgmt. gave no numeric 2027 guidance, but it still sees ESL and 
signage (especially mid-size) as the key drivers, while CE order visibility stays low. 
PTFE in focus on 3Q26 call; mass production likely in ’28 
E Ink’s PTFE collaboration with Taiflex (8039 TT; not covered) was one of the main 
topics on the 3Q26 call. We now take a neutral view on E Ink’s PTFE business. We see its 
strong R&D capability in material modification and strength in roll-to-roll manufacturing, 
but mgmt. said the business model is still under discussion and mass production could 
occur in 2028. As a result, our estimates currently do not include any PTFE contribution. 
3Q26 recap: less-favorable mix weighs on margins 
OP was 21%/17% below BofAe/consensus as GM/OPM of 52.2%/26.6% came in lower 
than expected (6.1ppt/7.0ppt below BofAe). Mgmt. said the weaker GM came from a 
higher mix of lower-margin module business. NP was 20%/15% below BofAe/consensus. 
Reiterate Neutral; cut PO to NT$148 on lower P/E 
We cut our 2026E EPS by 8% but lift our 2027-28E EPS by 0-5% to reflect 3Q26 miss 
and the latest guidance. We trim our PO to NT$148 (12x 2027E EPS) from NT$185 (15x 
2027E EPS), with a lower multiple on reduced CE demand visibility and macro risk. 12x is 
similar to ESL system integrators’ P/E average. We reiterate our Neutral given moderate 
sales growth, still-expanding margins, and fair valuation (Exhibit 4).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Electronics

Company Description 
E Ink is top global e-paper supplier with over 90% market 
share. E Ink was originally spun out of MIT Media Lab in 
1997 and was acquired by Prime View International in 
2009. The two companies were combined/branded as E Ink 
into e-paper supply chain. Besides its headquarters in 
Hsinchu (Taiwan), E Ink has facilities in Linkou (Taiwan), 
Yangzhou (China), and South Hadley (US).

Investment Rationale 
We have a Neutral rating on E Ink given moderate 2026 
guidance and 2027 sales growth with potential 
demand/order risk due to component shortage and macro 
uncertainty. Yet we still like E Ink's dominant position in 
global e-paper market with over 90% market share, high 
margins thanks to higher mix from better-margin IoT 
business, and positive OCF/FCF.

OBAL RESEARCH

Price objective basis & risk 
E Ink Holdings (PVWIF) 
We derive our PO of NT$148 from 12x 2027E EPS. Our target P/E multiple is set at the 
average of global key ESL system integrators. It is also at the lower end of 11-34x P/E 
range since AI era from 2023. We believe E Ink's valuation has been re-set with a lower 
premium after AI as its business is less directly exposed to AI.

Upside risks: (1) stronger market demand in consumer electronics, (2) faster ESL 
demand/adoption, (3) faster adoption and more applications in various vertical markets 
like smart transportation/city and smart medical, (4) more favorable USD/RMB 
movement to drive margin expansion and/or non-operating items, and (5) higher non-
operating royalty income due to stronger TFT-LCD demand.

Downside risks: (1) much weaker-than-expected consumer demand, (2) slower-than-
expected ESL demand/adoption to replace from conventional labels, (3) lower utilization 
rate to weigh on margins under higher capex and depreciation costs, (4) higher raw 
material cost and/or logistics cost, (5) lower non-operating royalty income due to weaker 
TFT-LCD demand, and (6) unfavorable FX movement (USD/RMB and/or USD/NTD) to 
weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be

股價相對大盤走勢

jpp-KY(5284 TT) Trading Buy

2027 年營運多點開花。 
投資建議：雖 jpp-KY 2026 年面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器
機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少的問題，但考量 2027 年航太
部門營運增速有望提升、伺服器部門在多款產品貢獻下，營運
仍能維持一定增速，使得 jpp-KY 2027 年營運多點開花，故給
予 jpp-KY Trading Buy 的投資建議。 
3Q26 雖有客戶規格調整因素、但電源機構件持續出貨，公告財
報表現佳：3Q26 因客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等
產品短期出貨較少，但整體仍受惠電源櫃結構件出貨，營運維
持高檔，使得 jpp-KY 於 11/06/2026 公告的 3Q26 財報表現佳，
3Q26 營收 11.64 億元，QoQ-3.26%，稅後純益 2.93 億元，
QoQ+25.62%， EPS 5.31 元。 
2026 年雖有客戶規格調整問題，但電源櫃產品出貨、歐洲子公
司整頓將至，營運維持高檔：2026 年 jpp 雖面臨客戶規格調整，
使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少，但電源櫃結構
件開始出貨，加上先前併購的第四家法國子公司 Segnere 整頓
預計於 4Q26 進入尾聲，開始提升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速，使
得 jpp 2026 年營運維持高檔，預估 2026 年營收 46.81 億元，
YoY+25.46%，稅後純益 9.24 億元，YoY+50.42%，EPS 16.73 元。 
2027 年航太、伺服器等部門業務推動，營運多點開花：2027 年
隨著歐洲航太營運部門開始成長，加上 2024 年與航太製造商-
Safran 的合作仍持續進行，研究部參考過往航太產業的產品開
發時程至少需 2-3 年，預期 2027 年起 Safran 等先前開發的產品
將逐步放量，推升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速。而在伺服器部門，
研究部認為規格調整等下游客戶的生產問題終將結束，在客戶
的生產問題結束後，加上 jpp 近年持續於伺服器產品的開發，
預期 jpp 2027 年的伺服器部門營運仍維持一定增速。綜合以上，
考量 jpp 2027 年有望呈現多產品、多部門的營運多點開花，預
估 2027 年營收 56.27 億元，YoY+20.21%，稅後純益 11.13 億
元，YoY+20.56%， EPS 20.17 元。

2 群益投顧

jpp-KY ESG：  
jpp-KY 依循 AA1000SES 利害關係人議合原則、GRI 準則編列 2025 年永
續報告書，並於 2024 年設置永續發展委員會，透過定期會議的召開，訂定
年度永續發展政策及與責任商業行為相關的承諾政策、並檢視各項永續發
展議。jpp-KY 透過安裝太陽能光電系統、節能設備，降低企業運營對環境
的影響，而經 ISO 14064-1: 2018 標準盤查的 2025 年溫室氣體排放量為
7,008.73 公噸 CO2e ，較 2024 年增加 12.61%；溫室氣體排放強度為 1.32，
較 2024 年減少 49.43%。此外，jpp-KY 認為企業的永續發展不僅僅是環保
層面的努力，也包括對員工與社會的承諾，所以 jpp-KY 重視員工的專業成
長與職涯發展，除了提供員工職能與過渡協助的教育訓練方案之外，也透過
定期績效評核制度，提供明確的回饋與發展建議，作為員工能力精進與升遷
發展的參考依據，同時透過建教合作計畫、舉辦全國性自動化比賽，與社會
建立起良性互動。jpp-KY 相信透過環保、員工福祉與社會責任方面的制度
化建立過程，將所有利害關係人納入企業長期行動、形成企業文化，過程中
不僅提升品牌形象，還能增強員工的忠誠度與使命感，也能為子孫後代創造
一個更可持續的世界。 
公司簡介： 
jpp-KY ( 經寶精密)成立於 1995 年，專精於航太、通訊(伺服器、自動販賣
機 )、醫療顯示等利基型板金市場。公司先前為了降低疫情對航太生產衝擊，
除了跨入航太 MRO 的維修市場，並且爭取到空中巴士的客機改貨機訂單，
也積極發展非航太業務，例如:AI 伺服器、自動販賣機、新能源機車等。2Q26
產品營收占比，航太 25%、通訊 65%、電子 5%、其他 5%。

3Q26 雖有客戶規格調整因素、但電源機構件持續出貨，公告財報表現佳：
3Q26 因客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出貨較少，但
整體仍受惠電源櫃結構件出貨，營運維持高檔，使得 jpp-KY 於 11/06/2026
公告的 3Q26 財報表現佳，3Q26 營收 11.64 億元，QoQ-3.26%，稅後純益
2.93 億元，QoQ+25.62%， EPS 5.31 元。

3 群益投顧 
4Q26 考量歐美航太淡季將至，營運維持高檔： 
4Q26 雖然電源櫃結構件仍會持續出貨，但研究部考量下游客戶電源產品生
產情形與歐美聖誕假期的航太淡季將至，預期 jpp 4Q26 營運仍維持高檔，
預估 4Q26 營收 11.47 億元，QoQ-1.51%，稅後純益 1.79 億元，QoQ-39.10%，
EPS 3.23 元。 
2026 年雖有客戶規格調整問題，但電源櫃產品出貨、歐洲子公司整頓將至，
營運維持高檔： 
2026 年 jpp 雖面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期出
貨較少，但電源櫃結構件開始出貨，加上先前併購的第四家法國子公司
Segnere 整頓預計於 4Q26 進入尾聲，開始提升 jpp 的航太部門營運增速，
使得 jpp 2026 年營運維持高檔，預估 2026 年營收 46.81 億元，YoY+25.46%，
稅後純益 9.24 億元，YoY+50.42%， EPS 16.73 元。 
圖二、jpp-KY 的 Rack 產品

資料來源：jpp-KY。  
2027 年航太、伺服器等部門業務推動，營運多點開花： 
2027 年隨著歐洲航太營運部門開始成長，加上 2024 年與航太製造商-Safran
的合作仍持續進行，研究部參考過往航太產業的產品開發時程至少需 2-3
年，預期 2027 年起 Safran 等先前開發的產品將逐步放量，推升 jpp 的航太
部門營運增速。而在伺服器部門，研究部認為規格調整等下游客戶的生產問
題終將結束，在客戶的生產問題結束後，加上 jpp 近年持續於伺服器產品的
開發，預期 jpp 2027 年的伺服器部門營運仍維持一定增速。綜合以上，考量
jpp 2027年有望呈現多產品、多部門的營運多點開花，預估 2027 年營收 56.27
億元，YoY+20.21%，稅後純益 11.13 億元，YoY+20.56%， EPS 20.17 元。 
投資建議： 
雖 jpp-KY 2026 年面臨客戶規格調整，使得伺服器機櫃、機構件等產品短期
出貨較少的問題，但考量 2027 年航太部門營運增速有望提升、伺服器部門
在多款產品貢獻下，營運仍能維持一定增速，使得 jpp-KY 2027 年營運多點
開花，故給予 jpp-KY Trading Buy 的投資建議。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
78 
삼성전자  (005930) 
2026.11.04

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 59%, 48%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
79

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급  
시가총액기준 산업별 시장비중대비 보유비중의 변화를 추천하는 것임 
 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

Document 268: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-14).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-14
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

### Page 3

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy

trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng

tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém

vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích 
FRT 148.70 1.2% 143.40 165.00 15.1% 
- Sau khi phủ sóng các thành phố lớn, chuỗi Long Châu đang mở rộng sang các vùng cấp 2, 3. 
Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,

Document 339: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 20261. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non-performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year 
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

Document 346: GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし ～2026年9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026年 11月 9日

1/3 
GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし 
～2026年 9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. 2026年 7～9月の収益率はマイナス 1.65％ 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）は 2026年 11月6日、同年 7～9月の運用実績を公表
した。期間収益率はマイナス 1.65％だった。7～9月は国内外の長期金利が上昇し、債券価格が下
落した。加えて円高も進み、外国資産の円換算額を押し下げた。資産別の収益率は国内債券がマ
イナス 1.80％、外国債券がマイナス 6.52％、国内株式がプラス 3.71％、外国株式がマイナス
1.86％となった。9月末の運用資産額は314兆3,876億円と 6月末から約 3.4兆円減少した。 
GPIFを巡っては、片山さつき財務・金融相が 7月に「日本の金融資産にさらなる投資をしてい
ただくという方向で後押しをする方策を追求したい」と発言し、ポートフォリオに変化があるか
注目が集まっていた。長期金利が上昇していたこともあり、特に国内債への関心が高かった。 
結論から言うと、国内債比率は基本ポートフォリオの 25％に極めて近く、運用姿勢の変化を読
み取ることは難しい。国内債の比率を引き上げるのではないかという観測は一旦後退するだろ
う。

2. 国内債券比率は 25％近辺を維持  
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは国内外の株式と債券をそれぞれ 25％とし、国内債券には±6ポイ
ントの乖離許容幅が設定されている。GPIFの公表資料によると、年金積立金全体に占める国内債
比率は 2026年9月末時点で 26.49％だった。これには年金特別会計で管理する積立金、約 5.4兆
円が含まれている。 
この積立金を国内債券と年金積立金全体から除き、GPIFの運用資産ベースで計算すると、9月末
の国内債券比率は25.23％となる。6月末の 24.98％から 0.25ポイント上昇したが、基本ポートフ
ォリオの25％をわずかに上回る水準だ（資料1）。 過去の実績と比較しても、今回の比率は突出し
ていない。2020～2025年度の 9月末平均は 25.05％で、2026年9月末はこれを0.18ポイント上回
る程度だ。

### Page 2

2/3 
資料 1 GPIFの運用資産構成比率（推計、2026年 6月末・9月末）

（注）年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いたGPIF運用資産ベース。 
（出所）GPIF「2026年度第 1四半期運用状況（速報）」、「同第 2四半期運用状況（速報）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成。

3. 国内債券の買越額は推計 1.4兆円  
次に、7～9月の国内債券への買越額を推計する。6月末と 9月末の国内債券残高の変化から、
期間中の運用損益による増減を除いて推計した。 
年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いた国内債券残高は、6月末の 79兆3,840億円から 9月末
の79兆3,206億円へ、634億円減少した。一方、7～9月の国内債券の運用損益はマイナス 1兆
4,452億円だった。この差額から、国内債券への買越額は約 1.4兆円と推計される（注 1）。4～6
月の約 5.7兆円と比較すると、国内債券への資金配分額は減少した（注2）。 
同じ方法で他の資産への資金配分を試算すると、外国債券は約 3.3兆円の買い越しとなった。
一方、国内株式は約2.0兆円、外国株式は約 0.8兆円の売り越しとなった。

4. 国内債券の運用姿勢に変化はみられず  
2026年7～9月の運用実績をみる限り、GPIFが国内債券の乖離許容幅を積極的に活用し、基本ポ
ートフォリオの25％を上回る水準へと比率を引き上げようとする動きは確認できなかった。 
国内債券は買い越しているとみられるものの、外国債券には国内債券を上回る資金が配分され
ている。4資産の比率はいずれも25％近い。基本ポートフォリオに近づけて運用する従来の姿勢
が維持されていると考えられる。 
以 上

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
【注釈】
1) 買越額は国内債券残高の変化から運用損益を控除した推計値。国内債券には為替ヘッジ付き外
国債券や円建て短期資産が含まれるため、日本国債の純購入額とは必ずしも一致しない。
2) 2026年7～9月は、GPIFの運用資産額の増減から運用収益額を差し引くと、約 1.9兆円の資金
流入があった計算になる。4～6月はほぼゼロだった。こうした資金流入の違いも、国内債券の
買越額を比較する際には考慮する必要がある。

Document 412: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15

Document 425: Turkey’s annual current account deficit narrows sharply

Source PDF: all report pdf/Turkey’s annual current account deficit narrows sharply.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

September data shows that Turkey's large current account
surplus is attributable to a moderation in domestic demand
and lower net gold imports. Its capital account, on the other
hand, saw outﬂows amid volatility in investment funds
Muhammet Mercan
Turkey's surplus widened in September to US$2.7bn, almost in line
with consensus. This is versus a mere $0.6bn in the same month
last year, narrowing the 12-month rolling current account deﬁcit to
$39.2bn – approximately 2.4% of GDP – from $41.3bn a month

ago.
A closer look at the monthly ﬁgures shows that the growing surplus
was primarily due to a lower trade gap, which improved from
-$5.4bn to -$3.6bn on the back of a) an increase in core trade
surplus and b) gold trade turning positive, despite a deterioration in
energy trade balance as the US-Iran war has pushed energy prices
up. Higher services income, more than oﬀsetting deterioration in

### Page 2

the primary and secondary income balances, also contributed to
the current account's rising surplus.
Breakdown of the current account (US$bn)
Source: CBT, ING
In the ﬁrst nine months of 2026, Turkey's current account deﬁcit
widened by $8.9bn compared with the same period in 2025,
reaching $28.0bn. The deterioration was driven mainly by a
$12.4bn narrowing of the core trade surplus and a $1.9bn widening
of the energy deﬁcit, partly oﬀset by a $3.0bn narrowing of the

gold deﬁcit and a $3.1bn higher services income.
On the capital account side, outﬂows continued at $13.7bn in
September, given the impact of investment fund volatility following
the weakness in August. With slight inﬂows from net errors and
omissions of $1.0bn, and considering the current account balance,
oﬃcial reserves depleted by $10.0bn.
Further analysis reveals that resident activity led to outﬂows
totalling $7.0bn, primarily from continuing acquisition of portfolio

assets and higher deposits abroad. Non-residents reduced their
Turkey exposure by cutting their investments in funds by $5.8bn
following the local fund shock and deposits held at local banks by
$1.1bn.
On the ﬂip side, net borrowing has continued with another $1.7bn
increase in September. In the breakdown of net borrowing,

corporates secured higher long-term ﬁnancing, while banks were

banks on a monthly basis, compared to 216% and 144%,

Breakdown of ﬁnancing (US$bn)
Source: CBT, ING

In the ﬁrst nine months of the year, resident outﬂows fell to $22bn
from $37.3bn a year ago. Foreign inﬂows, on the other hand,
recorded a jump, coming in at $40.5bn, compared to $33.1bn in
the same period of 2025. As a result, ﬂows in the capital account
almost tripled at $17.4bn, but remained well below the cumulative
current account deﬁcit.
In addition, outﬂows via net errors and omissions remained
elevated, totalling -$10.4 bn vs $1.9bn in inﬂows in 2025. Taken

together with the widening current account deﬁcit, which grew from
-$19.1bn to -$28bn, oﬃcial reserves were eroded by $20.9bn vs a
$11.5bn fall recorded a year earlier.

surplus is attributable to a moderation in domestic demand

重點摘要
• 國防需求已由短期補庫存轉
為多年期擴產
《國防需求已由短期補庫存轉為多年期擴產》
美國主要航空與國防類股公布3Q26財報，中東衝突
再升溫導致國防需求高於商業航空。以下是主要廠
商的分析 :
雷神∶受益於飛彈回補庫存，3Q26取得超過810億
美元商用與國防新訂單，其中國防訂單590億美元，
包括約230億美元「戰斧」飛彈訂單與140億美元
「先進中程空對空飛彈」（AMRAAM）訂單，帶
動總積壓訂單較上季增加520億美元，累計訂單
3,400億美元，年增36%。在飛彈防空系統與商業
航空維修服務市場強勁帶動下，公司上調2026年營
收到973億美元、EPS 7.70美元(年增22%)。
洛克希德馬丁∶受益於飛彈與火控系統產量提升，

帶動3Q26營收與獲利優於預期。在PAC-3多年期合
約則推升下，積壓訂單增加616億美元達2,920億美
元。由於美國、中東、北約國家飛彈補庫需求將持
續數年，所以公司上調2026年營收到810億美元(年
增8%)、EPS 30.55美元(年增42%)。
奇異航空∶受益於LEAP交付、備件、維修及shop
visit增加帶動，商用發動機及服務營收年增21%，
3Q26營收優於預期。在商用航空備件、維修服務及
引擎交付需求優於預期，加上主要供應商材料投入
改善， 公司上調2026 年營收到508億美元、EPS
7.75美元(年增26%)。
總結，雖然國防類股近期財報受益於產能提升維幅
上調全年財測，反觀油價高漲對於美國航空運輸業
造成全面性的衝擊，所以工業維持中立評等。

Document 496: コモディティ・レポート（2026年7～9月）

Source PDF: all report pdf/コモディティ・レポート（2026年7～9月）.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 37
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 20 日

調査レポート

コモディティ・レポート（2026 年 7～9 月）

調査部 主任研究員 芥田 知至 
1. コモディティ市況全般：原油が上昇傾向、銅は最高値更新、金は上値重い 
ドル建て国際商品市況全般の動向を示す FTSE・コアコモディティ CRB 指数は、2023 年 5 月 31 日に 2022
年 1 月以来の安値をつけた後、2026 年 3～9 月には大幅に上昇して 9 月 10 日に 2008 年 7 月以来の高値を
つけた。その後、10 月にかけてやや下落した。コモディティ相場は、中東情勢や米金融政策の行方を巡って、引
き続き、不安定な推移が見こまれる。

2. エネルギー市況：足元はブレントが一時 110 ドル、WTI が 100 ドル前後 
国際指標のブレント原油は、2026 年 4 月 30 日に 126.41 ドルと 2022 年 3 月以来の高値をつけた。足元は
110 ドル前後で不安定な動きとなっている。米国とイランは戦闘終結やホルムズ海峡再開に向けた動きは停滞し
たままであり、緊迫する中東情勢を受けて原油は高止まりが見こまれる。

3. ベースメタル市況：銅は 9 月上旬に 14,875 ドルの史上最高値 
銅相場は、2026 年 9 月 10 日に 14,875 ドルと史上最高値を記録し、足元は 14,000 ドル台半ばを中心に推
移している。銅は、原油高や景気悪化の影響が懸念される一方で、AI・エネルギー転換・防衛の関連分野を中
心に需要が伸びるとの見方が根強く、高値圏での一進一退が見こまれる。

4. 貴金属市況：金は 8 月中旬に 4,700 ドル近くまで持ち直すも足元は 4,100 ドル前後 
金は、2026 年 1 月 29 日に 5,594.82 ドルと史上最高値を記録した後、6 月 30 日には 3,942.99 ドルまで下
落し、足元は 4,100 ドル前後を中心に推移している。米利上げが続くとの観測が有力な中、中東情勢も安定化に
はまだ遠く、原油高が意識されて、金は買われにくい状況が続きやすいとみられる。

原油・銅・金相場の騰落率（2026 年 9 月末の 2026 年 6 月末との対比）
